Consulting the Table of &chi;&sup2; Critical Values and a <span style='color: #8601af;'>level of significance (&alpha;)</span>=0.50, we obtain a critical value of 2.37.</p><p>Since the <span style="color: #935d00;">chi-square statistic (&chi;<sup>2</sup>)</span> of 19.13 is greater than the critical value of 2.37, the <span style="color: #0247fe;">null hypothesis (H<sub>0</sub>)</span> has BEEN REJECTED.</p><p>Your lab partner completed a chi-square (&chi;<sup>2</sup>) test on your lab data (above) for the F<sub>2</sub> generation in a standard dihybrid cross. The goal was to verify if the observed results matched the expected phenotype ratios.</p><p>However, it appears they made an error. <strong>What did they do wrong?</strong></p>	The expected progeny for the <span style="color: #0247fe;">null hypothesis (H<sub>0</sub>)</span> is incorrect.
